1900.] NEWOHWANG. CUSTOM HOUSE, NEWORWANG, 15Tm GOR 19.o SIR, I.-The Imperial Russian Provisional Civil Administration having opened on the i2th August, a Custem House to collect, under Foreign supervision, Dues and Likin on Junk Cargoes, I now transmit the statistics of that trade from date of opening to 3oth September 19oo. On all cargoes arriving or leaving by sea-going junks Dues and Duties were collected at Treaty Tariff rates, and Likin at the rate of 4 per cent. ad valorem on Imports and A per cent. ad valorem on Exports. Boats trading up river paid Tonnage Dues, but no Dities were levied on their eargoes. II. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the third quarter of the year i19o0. b. The Custom House (M $ ) was opened on the ist July 1899. The collection of Tariff Import and Export Duty and,Convention Likii on Opium (HTh 30 + 8o per picul), and the collection of Native Duties and Likin on Junk Cargoes at half Tariff rates, commenced on that day. The stations at present provisionally controlled by the Kiaochow Customs are known as Taputon ( P f f); Hungshihyai (* &#39; g); Lingshanwei ( (f )including Tachiangkon ( ); Nuku (A ); Tsanghou (a Ql); and Shatszkou( J U) including Tengyao (M W). c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China, the statistics of goods landed aid consumed in German territory not being included. 1900.] YOCiHOW. 51 (Received i8th October 1900.) CUSTOM HOUSE, YOCHOW, 1on OcToBER 1900. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the third quarter of the year 19oo. b. PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended September 30, 1900.
222 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JULY-SEPT., 1900.] NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 351. SWATOW DISTRICT. REFERRING to Notice to Mariners No. 349, dated the i ith June igoo, notice is hereby given that the neighbourhood of the position therein given: &quot;East Point, Namoa Island, N. true; Three Chimney Bluff Point, S. 700 W.:&quot; where the s.s. Kveili-a reported having struck in 14 feet of water, has been carefully sounded, and swept with weighted lines, and no obstruction whatever found, th6 least depth obtained being 12 fathoms. The local fishermen were also offered a liberal reward if they could point out any uncharted danger in the vicinity, but they declared that none existed. SHANGHAI, ioth August 1000.

236 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. JULY-SEPT., MPERIAL ARITIME USTOMS. MOVEMENTS IN THE SERVI CE. (Gazette No. 6.r6.) From 1st June to 31st August 1900. SPEOIAL SECTION: PEKING STAFF. I90. June 9 All employ4s-Customs, Postal, and Educational-abandoned their separate outlying houses, in accord- ance with the Inspector General&#39;s instructions, and took refuge at the Inspectorate General for mutual protection against Boxer attack. Co-operation arranged for with the neighbouring Legation Guards-Austrian, French, and Japanese. 13 Boxers entered the city and set fire to various chapels and churches. Precautions redoubled. i9 A Yamin red letter informed the Inspector General that the Foreign demand for the surrender of the Taku Forts was tantamount to a declaration of war, and that therefore all the Foreign Representa- tives had been ordered to quit Peking witWn 24 hours, i.e., before 4 P.m., Wednesday, 20th June. Got off a message to Tientsin. 2o The Inspector General answered YamAn note, and said that under the circumstances it appeared expedient for the Customs Staff to accompany the Legations, and as the Inspectorate buildings contained 40 years important archives and many valuable books, etc., requested the Yan6n to depute officers and men to take charge of them and their contents. The bearer of this reply was riding behind the German Minister, but fled back to the Inspectorate when Baron voN KETTELER was shot, about 10 A.M., and thus the letter did not reach the Yamn. The Austrians abandoned their Legation at 3 P.M., and consequently the Inspectorate was vacated and all the Customs Staff took refuge in the British Legation, where a separate building was most obligingly set apart for their use. At 4 P.m. fing began and the siege commenced. Despatched another message to the Commissioner, Tientsin. The Customs refugees in the British Legation were the following:- I.-REVENUE DEPARTMENT. 1o. Communication with the South having been reopened, I shall resume duty on the 22nd instant, and the siege being happily at end, the following Staff arrangements are now made (a.) MM. BREDON, PiRY, and BRAZIER are granted whatever leave famUy requirements demand. (b.) MM. OLIVER, RUSSELL, and MEARs are granted a year&#39;s leave each, the last on full pay after 20 years continuous service; and leave for three months each is granted to MM. RICHARDSON, BETHELL, BISMAROK, DIEHR, DR PINNA, and OREGLIA (to report for instructions to the Commissioner of Customs at either Shanghai or Kowloon). (C.) MM. WINTOUR, SMYTH, SimpsoN, and DR COURCY are placed at the disposal of His Ex- cellency the British Minister for special duty. (d.) MM. BREWITT-TAYLOR, Dr LucA, and LAURU are to proceed on transfer to Swatow, Chungking, and Shanghai respectively. (e.) MM. MAcouN (Acting Audit Secretary), GRANZELLA (Acting Assistant Secretary), KoNo- VALOFF (Acting Private Secretary), voN STRAUCH, SANDERCOOCK, and DESTELAN are to remain in Peking for duty in the Inspectorate, and MM. FRGusoN, ENCARNAgiO, and DUPREE for Postal work. (f) Customs and Postal work will be carried on in the j W , opposite the % fp], on the AF k (g.) MM. GRANZELLA, MACOUN, and FERGUSON will make the necessary arrangements for carrying on the work of the General Office, Accounts Office, and Post Office. (Signed) ROBERT HART, Inspector General. 240 CUSTOMS GAZETTE, [JULY-SEPT., 1900. 3.-Mr. voN STRAUCH&#39;S services have been of a most distinguished nature, and most willingly and ably rendered; his appointment is confirmed, and he is promoted to be 3rd Assistant, A. 4.-Mr. W. DUPRE, of the Postal Department, is transferred to the Revenue Department and promoted to be 4th Assistant, A, in special recognition of his gallantry as a Customs volunteer and on various- outside expeditions, and for other valuable work done by him in the general interest. 5.--Mr. IKUTARO SUGI, of the Educational Department, Professor of Japanese in the T&#39;ung Wen Kwan, did excellent work as a Japanese volunteer in the defence of the St WANG f1, in obtaining intelligence, and in getting in supplies; in recognition of which and his personal bravery he is transferred to the Revenue Department and promoted to be a 3rd Assistant, A. 6.-Apart from wounds or injuries, more or less severe, sustained while on duty during the siege by MM. RICHARDSON, DE LUCA, MACOUN, DESTELAN, DE CouRcY, and WINTOUR, the Staff is fortunate in having to deplore the loss of only one of its members, the gallant Mr. E. WAGNER, Acting Postal Secretary, killed by a shell on the ist July while assisting in the defence of the French Legation. 7-Hereto is appended copy of a letter from His Excellency the British Minister to Mr. voN STRAUCH&#39;S address, thanking the Customs volunteers for their services, a recognition and appreciation which the Staff will be glad to see recorded. (Signed) ROBERT HART, Tnspector General. APPEND. His Excellency the British Minister to Mr. E. A. W. voN STRAuam. BRITISH LEGATION, PEKING, i9th August 1900. DEAR Mn. VON STRAUCH, I nE that you will convey to the Customs volunteers who have been under your command during the recent siege my high appreciation of the valuable services they have rendered to myself and the community in general in the successful defence of the Legation. It has been a very geat pleasure to me to observe the readiness and alacrity with which my orders have been received and the smartness and gallantry with which they have been executed. Believe me, Yours very truly, (Signed) CLAUDE M. MACDONALD. APPENDIX . KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. JULY-SEPTEMBER 1000.
254 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [J U LY-SEPT.,. (Received 18th October 19oo.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOQN, 13TH OCTOBER 1900. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the third quarter of the year 1900, July-September. b. The Custom House (tL f ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Th 30 and Hk.2k 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. The statipns then controlled by the Kowloon Customs were known as Capsuimoon (M 7k PI), Chang*chow (g ) Fotochow ( R-A Y), and Kowloon (A &quot;). On the 5th October 1899, as a result of the extension of territory to form part of the Colony of Hongkong, they were removed to Taishan (* J), Lintini (fT &#39;%f ), and Shamchun (~ fJ;), on the north and west; to Saniun (2 f), on the east; and to Shaiichung (ff on the Mirs Bay littoral. The Custom House is now called 1ft M - c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these stntis tics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II. 264 CUSTOMS GAZETTR [JULY-SEPT. (Beceived i8th October i9oo.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, ITH OCTOBER 1900. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the third quarter of the year 1900, July-September. b. The Custom House (A 4[ W) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (H1k.Z&amp;amp; 30 and &amp;amp;k.2W 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow (A ), Tungho (X N), and Chienshan ( j oift) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (5 ), Kuttai (W *) and Kwanchiap ( P) sub-stations. c..The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China.
